<div class='question question'>

What is the remainder when:

$743589 \times 301647$ is divided by $5$ ?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {743589 \times 301647} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {224301391083}{5} \\\\
&= 44860278216.6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Sean scored $27$ out of $45$ in a test. Write this as a percentage.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Percentage} &= \dfrac {27} {45} \times 100 \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {27 \times 100} {45} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {9 \times 3 \times 5 \times 20} {9 \times 5} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 3 \times \cancel {5} \times 20} {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                   &= 3 \times 20 \\\\
                   &= 60 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Percentage} &= \dfrac {27} {45} \times 100 \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {27 \times 100} {45} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {9 \times 3 \times 5 \times 20} {9 \times 5} \\\\
                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {9} \times 3 \times \cancel {5} \times 20} {\cancel {9} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                   &= 3 \times 20 \\\\
                   &= 60 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In a class of $30$ pupils, $21$ are girls. What percentage of the class is boys?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of boys}      &= 30 - 21 \\\\
                            &= 9 \\\\
\text {\% of boys}          &= \dfrac {9} {30} \times 100 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {9 \times 100} {30} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 3 \times 10 \times 10} {3 \times 10} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {3} \times 10 \times \cancel {10}} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                            &= 30 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$30 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of boys}      &= 30 - 21 \\\\
                            &= 9 \\\\
\text {\% of boys}          &= \dfrac {9} {30} \times 100 \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {9 \times 100} {30} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times 3 \times 10 \times 10} {3 \times 10} \\\\
                            &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {3} \times 10 \times \cancel {10}} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
                            &= 30 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$30 \%$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<div class='workings'>

&= 0.8 \\\\
&= \dfrac {8} {10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2 \times 4} {2 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 4} {\cancel {2} \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {4} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

$\dfrac {4} {5}$

&= \dfrac {2 \times 4} {2 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 4} {\cancel {2} \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {4} {5}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

&= \dfrac {17} {20}
\end{aligned}

&= \dfrac {17 \times \cancel {5}} {20 \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
&= \dfrac {17} {20}
\end{aligned}
$

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Rearrange the following in order of size, **smallest to largest**.

$\dfrac{9}{25}, 0.371, \dfrac{2}{5}, 38\%$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

To compare, we can convert all the numbers to fractions, decimals or percentages. 

Let's convert all the numbers into decimals.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5},  \quad 38 \% \\\\
&= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {25 \times 4}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2 \times 2} {5 \times 2},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {36} {100}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {4} {10},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371,  \quad 0.4,  \quad 0.38
\end{aligned}
$

Let's put them in ascending order now.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371, \quad 0.38, \quad 0.4 \\\\
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371, \quad 38 \%,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371, \quad 38 \%,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>To compare, we can convert all the numbers to fractions, decimals or percentages. 

Let's convert all the numbers into decimals.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5},  \quad 38 \% \\\\
&= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {25 \times 4}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {2 \times 2} {5 \times 2},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {36} {100}, \quad 0.371,  \quad \dfrac {4} {10},  \quad \dfrac {38} { 100} \\\\
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371,  \quad 0.4,  \quad 0.38
\end{aligned}
$

Let's put them in ascending order now.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 0.36, \quad 0.371, \quad 0.38, \quad 0.4 \\\\
&= \dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371, \quad 38 \%,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {9} {25}, \quad 0.371, \quad 38 \%,  \quad \dfrac {2} {5}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

$

\text{The prime factors for 66, 30 are:}\\\\
66&=2\times3\times11\\\\

\text{The factors that given numbers have in common are:}\\\\
&=2\times3\\\\
&=6
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The prime factors for 12, 8 are:}\\\\
12&=2\times2\times3\\\\
8&=2\times 2\times2\\\\
\text{Multiply each factor the greater number of times}\\
\text{it occurs in steps i) or ii) above to find the LCM:}\\\\
&=2\times2\times2\times 3\\\\
&=24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 3 \times 2^3 \\\\
&= 3 \times 8 \\\\
&= 24
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
&= 2 \times 3^2 \\\\
&= 2 \times 9 \\\\
&= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Larger number $=3 \times 2^3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$=3 \times 2^3$

</div>

&= 2 \times 9 \\\\
&= 18
\end{aligned}
$

Larger number $=3 \times 2^3$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8A284E0B-57E3-41DE-ABA6-67932AE4157D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Circle the numbers which are larger than $1\diagup3$

$0.25\:\:\:  2\diagup9\:\:\:  0.039\:\:\:  4\diagup5\:\:\:  3\diagup8$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let's convert all fractions into decimals.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{1}{3} \\\\
&= 0.33 \\\\
&= 0.25, \dfrac{2}{9}, 0.039, \dfrac{4}{5}, \dfrac{3}{8}\\\\
&= 0.25, 0.222, 0.039, 0.80, 0.375
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Larger numbers}   &= 0.80, 0.375\\\\
                        &= \dfrac{4}{5}, \dfrac{3}{8}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac{4}{5}, \dfrac{3}{8}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let's convert all fractions into decimals.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac{1}{3} \\\\
&= 0.33 \\\\
&= 0.25, \dfrac{2}{9}, 0.039, \dfrac{4}{5}, \dfrac{3}{8}\\\\
&= 0.25, 0.222, 0.039, 0.80, 0.375
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Larger numbers}   &= 0.80, 0.375\\\\
                        &= \dfrac{4}{5}, \dfrac{3}{8}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

<div class='question question'>

Find the missing digit to make the calculation correct:

$4\:\:\:A$

$\dfrac{\times\:\:\:\:A}{3\:\:\:2\:\:\:9}$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
       &   4   &   7 \\
\times &       &   7 \\
\hline
   3   &   2   &   9
\end{array}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
       &   4   &   7 \\
\times &       &   7 \\
\hline
   3   &   2   &   9
\end{array}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-01B71FE1-4A60-4BF3-8381-0E7963EDDE0A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Subtract three times the smallest number from twice the largest number in this list:

$4, \quad 11, \quad 8, \quad 2, \quad 7, \quad 13$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \times 13 - 3 \times 2 \\\\
&= 26 - 6 \\\\
&= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \times 13 - 3 \times 2 \\\\
&= 26 - 6 \\\\
&= 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

&= 6
\end{aligned}
$

\end{aligned}
$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C568D9CE-2255-4775-8B6E-7406773C0CCD'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Calculate $45412 \times 834 + 45412 \times 162 + 45412 \times 2$. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 45412 \times 834 + 45412 \times 162 + 45412 \times 2 \\\\
&= 45412 \times \left( 834 + 162 + 2 \right) \\\\
&= 45412 \times 998 \\\\
&= 45412 \times (1000 - 2) \\\\
&= 45412 \times 1000 - 45412 \times 2 \\\\
&= 45412000 - 90824 \\\\
&= 45321176
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$45321176$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 45412 \times 834 + 45412 \times 162 + 45412 \times 2 \\\\
&= 45412 \times \left( 834 + 162 + 2 \right) \\\\
&= 45412 \times 998 \\\\
&= 45412 \times (1000 - 2) \\\\
&= 45412 \times 1000 - 45412 \times 2 \\\\
&= 45412000 - 90824 \\\\
&= 45321176
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

$\dfrac{4}{7}-\dfrac{3}{5}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {4} {7} - \dfrac {3} {5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {4 \times 7} {7 \times 5} - \dfrac {3 \times 7} {5 \times 7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {28} {35} - \dfrac {21} {35} \\\\
&= \dfrac {28 - 21} {35} \\\\
&= \dfrac {7} {35} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {7}} {\cancel {7} \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

&= \dfrac {28} {35} - \dfrac {21} {35} \\\\
&= \dfrac {28 - 21} {35} \\\\
&= \dfrac {7} {35} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel {7}} {\cancel {7} \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {1} {5}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$2\dfrac{1}{3} \times 3\dfrac{3}{7}$ 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2\dfrac {1} {3} \times 3\dfrac {3} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {7} {3} \times \dfrac {24} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {7 \times 24} {3 \times 7}  \\\\
&= \dfrac {7 \times 8 \times 3} {3 \times 7}  \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{7} \times 8 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3} \times \cancel{7}}  \\\\
&= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2\dfrac {1} {3} \times 3\dfrac {3} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {7} {3} \times \dfrac {24} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {7 \times 24} {3 \times 7}  \\\\
&= \dfrac {7 \times 8 \times 3} {3 \times 7}  \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{7} \times 8 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel{3} \times \cancel{7}}  \\\\
&= 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

What is the largest whole number that is a factor of (i.e. will divide into) $357$ and $255$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Find the prime factorization of 357

= $3\times7\times17$

Find the prime factorization of 255

= $3\times5\times17$

To find the GCF, multiply all the prime factors common to both numbers:

= $3\times 17$

= $51$

</div>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$51$

</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the smallest whole number that $357$ and $255$ will divide into?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Find the prime factorization of 357

= $3\times7\times17$

Find the prime factorization of 255

= $3\times5\times17$

Multiply each factor the greater number of times it occurs in steps i) or ii) above to find the LCM:

= $3\times5\times7\times 17$

= $1785$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate $231 \times 43 - 31 \times 43$ using an efficient method. Show working.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 231 \times 43 - 31 \times 43 \\\\
&= 43 \times \left( 231 - 31 \right) \\\\
&= 43 \times 200 \\\\
&= 8600
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8600$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\begin{aligned}
&= 231 \times 43 - 31 \times 43 \\\\
&= 43 \times \left( 231 - 31 \right) \\\\
&= 43 \times 200 \\\\
&= 8600
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Calculate $26 \times 345 - 101 \times 345 + 75 \times 345$ using an efficient method. Show working.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 26 \times 345 - 101 \times 345 + 75 \times 345 \\\\
&= 345 \times (26 - 101 + 75) \\\\
&= 345 \times (101 - 101) \\\\
&= 345 \times 0  \\\\
&= 0
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 26 \times 345 - 101 \times 345 + 75 \times 345 \\\\
&= 345 \times (26 - 101 + 75) \\\\
&= 345 \times (101 - 101) \\\\
&= 345 \times 0  \\\\
&= 0
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Using BIDMAS
,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 6 + 7 - 9 + 3 - 12 - 2 \\\\
&= 13 - 9 + 3 - 12 - 2 \\\\
&= 4 + 3 - 12 - 2 \\\\
&= 7 - 12 - 2 \\\\
&= - 5 - 2 \\\\
&= - 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

&= 4 + 3 - 12 - 2 \\\\
&= 7 - 12 - 2 \\\\
&= - 5 - 2 \\\\
&= - 7
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (9 \times 5) \div (2 + 1) \\\\
&= 45 \div 3 \\\\
&= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$3\times  7 - 6 \times 4 - 3 = 15$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$(3 \times  7) - (6 \times (4 - 3)) = 15$

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (3 \times 7 ) - (6 \times  (4 - 3)) \\\\
&= 21 - 6 \\\\
&= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$((3 \times  7) - (6 \times (4 - 3)))$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

$

\end{aligned}

                                         &= 66.23 \text{p} \\\\

\end{aligned}

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The total length of seven cars is $22.19 \ \text{metres}$. What is the average length of these cars?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average length of cars} &= \dfrac {22.19} {7} \\\\
&= \dfrac {22.19\times100} {7\times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2219} {7\times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {7\times 317} {7\times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{7}\times 317} {\cancel{7}\times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {317} {100} \\\\
                              &= 3.17 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3.17 \ \text{metres}$

</div>
</div>

&= \dfrac {22.19\times100} {7\times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {2219} {7\times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {7\times 317} {7\times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel{7}\times 317} {\cancel{7}\times 100} \\\\
&= \dfrac {317} {100} \\\\
                              &= 3.17 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out $11 + 39 \div 13 - 5 \times 3$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Using BIDMAS
,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 11 + 39 \div 13 - 5 \times 3 \\\\
&= 11 + 3 - 5\times 3 \\\\
&= 11 + 3 - 15 \\\\
&= 14 -15\\\\
&=-1
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$-1$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_bidmas,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 11 + 39 \div 13 - 5 \times 3 \\\\
&= 11 + 3 - 5\times 3 \\\\
&= 11 + 3 - 15 \\\\
&= 14 -15\\\\
&=-1
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$67\%$ of all pencils manufactured are never used up.

In a bulk pack of $3490$ pencils, how many will not be used up?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pencils never used up}          &= 67 \% \times 3490 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {67} {100} \times 3490 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {67 \times 3490} {100} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {233830} {100} \\\\
                                                &= 2338.3 \\\\
\text{Rounding down to nearest whole number}    &= 2338
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2338$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of pencils never used up}          &= 67 \% \times 3490 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {67} {100} \times 3490 \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {67 \times 3490} {100} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {233830} {100} \\\\
                                                &= 2338.3 \\\\
\text{Rounding down to nearest whole number}    &= 2338
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2338$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-49A4C1C8-A26A-4008-B8AD-ED8A462A2B09'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

When three consecutive odd numbers are multiplied together, the result is $9177$.

What is the sum of the numbers? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the first number be a, so consecutive odd numbers will be a + 2 and a + 4

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times ( a + 2 ) \times ( a + 4 )                         &= 9177 \\\\
a                                                           &= \sqrt[3]{9177} \\\\
a                                                           &= 21 \, \text{approx.}
\end{aligned}
$

|    Numbers     |  Product  |
|----------------|-----------|
| $17, 19, 21$   |  $6783$   |
| $19, 21, 23$   |  $9177$   |
| $21, 23, 25$   |  $12075$  |

So the numbers are $= 19 , 21 , 23$

Sum of these numbers are $= 19 + 21 + 23 = 63$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$63$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the first number be a, so consecutive odd numbers will be a + 2 and a + 4

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times ( a + 2 ) \times ( a + 4 )                         &= 9177 \\\\
a                                                           &= \sqrt[3]{9177} \\\\
a                                                           &= 21 \, \text{approx.}
\end{aligned}
$

|    Numbers     |  Product  |
|----------------|-----------|
| $17, 19, 21$   |  $6783$   |
| $19, 21, 23$   |  $9177$   |
| $21, 23, 25$   |  $12075$  |

So the numbers are $= 19 , 21 , 23$

Sum of these numbers are $= 19 + 21 + 23 = 63$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$63$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>


</div>
</li>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$1 \div 2 - 4 \div 6$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: Using BIDMAS
,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 1 \div 2 - 4 \div 6 \\\\
&= \dfrac{1}{2} - \dfrac{4}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{1 \times 3} {2 \times 3} - \dfrac{4} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3} {6} - \dfrac{4} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 - 4}{6} \\\\
&= -\dfrac{1}{6}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$-\dfrac{1} {6}$

</div>
</div>

\begin{aligned}
&= 1 \div 2 - 4 \div 6 \\\\
&= \dfrac{1}{2} - \dfrac{4}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{1 \times 3} {2 \times 3} - \dfrac{4} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3} {6} - \dfrac{4} {6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{3 - 4}{6} \\\\
&= -\dfrac{1}{6}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$(9 \times 5) \div (2 + 1) = 15$

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (9 \times 5) \div (2 + 1) \\\\
&= 45 \div 3 \\\\
&= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

&= 15
\end{aligned}

$3 \times 7 - 6 \times 4 - 3 = 15$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$(3 \times  7) - (6 \times (4 - 3)) = 15$

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= (3 \times 7 ) - (6 \times  (4 - 3)) \\\\
&= 21 - 6 \\\\
&= 15
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

\end{aligned}
